L1833: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1834: The final popPK model was determined to be reasonably well describe the observed PK profile of zenocutuzumab. None of the tested covariates were considered to be clinically meaningful to warrant dosing adjustment for application of zenocutuzumab.
L1835: No clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of zenocutuzumab were observed based on age (22 to 88 years), sex, race [White or Asian], body weight (38 to 126 kg), albumin level (20 to 49 g/L), mild or moderate renal impairment (creatinine clearance (CLcr) 30 to 89 mL/min), and mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt;1 to 1.5 times ULN or AST &gt; ULN).
L1836: Black patients were removed from the race group as insufficient number of black patients were included in the popPK model. Zenocutuzumab PK was studied and compared in limited number of cancer types. Therefore, data are not sufficient to support the statement of no effect of tumor type on exposure.
L1837: ## 19.4.2. Exposure-Response Analysis
L1838: ## 19.4.2.1. ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
L1839: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1840: ## Efficacy:
L1841: - The Cavg,ss could not be identified as a significant predictor for the duration of response (DOR) per central assessment. Per local assessment, the Cavg,ss was found to be a borderline significant predictor for the duration of response: after one year of treatment, more than 50% of the patients in the upper two Cavg,ss quartiles were still responders, while this was lower than 25% for the lower two Cavg,ss quartiles.
- The response was found to be significantly higher for pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma (PDAC) patients versus patients with tumor types other than PDAC and non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) (overall response rate (ORR) central and local), and for eastern cooperative oncology group performance status (ECOG) 0 patients.
- For ORR central endpoint, it was found that the ER relationship is partially driven by early drop out patients who received only a few doses and thus did not reach their potential Cavg,ss or timepoint of first imaging assessment. Additionally, an ER relationship was also observed with individual clearance as a predictor. It is thus expected that the ER
L1842: relationship for efficacy endpoints may be confounded by interaction between patient health factors, antibody clearance, and response status. It was anticipated that severely ill patients, with an unfavorable prognosis, could have a faster drug clearance on average. In the present analysis, patients with a higher clearance were associated with a low albumin-to-alkaline phosphatase ratio, which was reported as a potential biomarker for poor prognosis and tumor recurrence. Therefore, interpretation of efficacy ER relationship should be cautious due to confounding and data limitation from one dose level.
L1843: ## 19.4.2.2. ER (efficacy) Assessment Summary
L1844: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1845: Exposure-response analyses for overall response, clinical benefit rate, duration of response and maximum percentage decrease from baseline in sum of diameters of target lesions supported that a fixed dose of 750 mg Q2W is an adequate dose for clinical use of zenocutuzumab in NRG1+ cancer patients. Please see section 6.3.2.1 for a summary of the key results.
L1846: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	The objective of this analysis was to perform an E-R analysis for safety and efficacy dependent variables of interest. Different individual exposure metrics derived from the final population PK model for zenocutuzumab were evaluated as predictors of response, together with other, compound-independent, intrinsic and extrinsic factors. A formal E-R analysis plan describing the analysis steps was established prior to receiving the final locked dataset.
Study Included	MCLA-128-CL01.
Endpoint	Primary: overall response rate (ORR), clinical benefit rate (CBR), duration of response (DOR) and maximum percentage decrease from baseline in sum of diameters of target lesions (sum of lesions nadir). For each endpoint the local and central assessment was evaluated.
No. of Patients (total, and with individual PK)	129 patients, all with individual PK.
Population Characteristics (Table 57 and Table 58)	General	Population characteristics were summarized for the total population, consisting of the safety and efficacy population.
Age 61 (21 - 88) years.
Weight 67 (38.4 – 125.5)kg
132 (40%) male, 198 (60%) female
238 (72%) caucasian, 67 (20%) Asian, 12 (3.6%) not reported, 7 (2.1%) other, 6 (1.8%) black or African American.
L1847: Pediatrics (if any)	Not included
Dose(s) Included	750 mg Q2W
Exposure Metrics Explored (range)	Average serum concentration at steady state (C_{\text{avg,ss}}). Median 99.9 (range 35.9 – 174) µg/mL.
Covariates Evaluated	Age, sex, baseline bodyweight, race, ECOG status, liver enzyme category, tumour tpe, sum of target lesion diameters, presence of visceral lesions, number of organs involved, number of previous lines of therapy.
Final Model Parameters	Summary	Acceptability [FDA's comments]
Model Structure	
For ORR and CBR a logistic regression model was applied:
\text{logit}(P) = \log_e\left(\frac{P}{1-P}\right) \approx \text{intercept} + \text{slope} \cdot \text{exposure}
where P is the predicted probability of response, intercept is the estimated probability of response when the exposure metric is zero, and the slope is the estimated slope of the linear relationship with exposure.
For DOR a Kaplan-Meier evaluation was performed.
For sum of lesions nadir a linear regression model was applied:
DV \approx \text{intercept} + \text{slope} \cdot \text{exposure}
where intercept is the estimated sum of lesions when exposure metric is zero and slope is the estimated slope of the linear relationship with exposure.
Acceptable
Model Parameter Estimates	ORR & CBR: Table 59
Sum of lesions nadir: Table 60	Acceptable (CBR and Sum of lesions nadir are NOT primary clinical endpoints and are NOT assessed.)
Model Evaluation	For ORR, CBR and sum of lesions nadir, assessed central and locally, the relationship with	Acceptable (CBR and
L1848: C_{avg,ss} was significant in univariate analyses and maintained significance in multivariate analyses.
For central assessed DOR C_{avg,ss} was not found a significant predictor, and for locally assessed DOR C_{avg,ss} was found to be a borderline significant predictor.
Sum of lesions nadir are NOT primary clinical endpoints and are NOT assessed.)
Covariates and Clinical Relevance	
The probability for central and locally assessed ORR was slightly higher for PDAC patients vs patients with tumor types other than PDAC and NSCLC.
The probability for central assessed CBR was higher for males vs females, patients with ECOG 0 and PDAC vs ECOG 1 or 2 and other than PDAC tumor patients. For locally assessed CBR the same effects of sex and tumor types were observed, but the effect of ECOG was not significant.
For central and locally assessed DOR covariates other than exposure were not considered.
The decrease in central and locally assessed sum of lesions nadir is higher in PDAC patients versus patients with tumours other than PDAC and NSCLC, and lower for patients with four or more prior lines of therapy vs 2-3 therapies. In addition, for locally assessed sum of lesions nadir, males were found to have a higher effect.
Acceptable (CBR and Sum of lesions nadir are NOT primary clinical endpoints and are NOT assessed.)
Simulation for Specific Population	Not applicable	Acceptable

Figure 19	Acceptable
Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	
Higher exposure is associated with higher ORR, CBR and sum of lesions nadir. In additional analysis, an ER relationship was also observed with individual clearance (independent of the individual dosing history) as a predictor.
These positive trends for E-R would indicate the possibility of achieving higher benefit with higher Q2W exposures, although there were limitations in
Acceptable (CBR and Sum of lesions nadir are NOT primary clinical endpoints and are NOT assessed.)
L1849: interpreting these analyses because of potential confounding by patient health factors.

12.2 Pharmacodynamics	The exposure-response relationship and time-course of pharmacodynamic response for the effectiveness of zenocutuzumab have not been fully characterized.

L1866: ## 19.4.2.3. ER (safety) Executive Summary
L1867: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1868: ## Safety:
L1869: None of the exposure metrics (average concentration under steady state (C_{avg,ss}) and maximum concentration under steady state (C_{max,ss}) (log and not transformed) were significant predictors for the occurrence of treatment related adverse event (TRAE) grade \ge 3, TRAE any grade, diarrhea and left ventricular ejection fraction (LVEF) reduction.
L1870: The C_{avg,ss} was found to be a significant predictor for the occurrence of Any AE grade \ge 3 whereby the probability of this event decreased with increasing C_{avg,ss}. A Kaplan-Meier (KM) analysis was performed for the time to first occurrence of this event. In this analysis, the C_{avg,ss} and the dosing interval were found to be significant predictors for the time to this event. In line with the trends observed in the logistic regression analysis, within the first year, the strongest drop in fraction of patients without this event was observed for the patients with a C_{avg,ss} below the median of the population.
L1871: For infusion related reaction (IRR) of the first dose, the planned analysis was considered not to be informative, because the occurrence of this event triggered the interruption of the infusion, thus leading to a lower C_{max,1st} on average by design.
L1872: The C_{avg,ss} and C_{max,ss} quartiles, and the dosing interval were found to be significant predictors for the time to first occurrence of a dose interruption. However, the causal relationship for this observation remains unclear, since dose interruption directly leads to a lower exposure.
L1873: ## 19.4.2.4 ER (safety) Assessment Summary
L1874: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1875: Exposure-response analyses for occurrence of a treatment related adverse event of grade 3 or higher, occurrence of an adverse event irrespective of causality of grade 3 or higher, occurrence of any treatment related adverse event, occurrence of an infusion related reaction after the first dose, occurrence of diarrhea and occurrence of clinically significant left ventricular ejection fraction reduction showed no or a negative relationship with exposure to zenocutuzumab. Please see section 6.3.2.1 for a summary of the key results.
L1876: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	The objective of this analysis was to perform an E-R analysis for safety and efficacy dependent variables of interest. Different individual exposure metrics derived from the final population PK model for zenocutuzumab were
L1877: ## Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
L1878: BLA 761352
L1879: BIZENGRI (zenocutuzumab)
L1880: evaluated as predictors of response, together with other, compound-independent, intrinsic and extrinsic factors. A formal E-R analysis plan describing the analysis steps was established prior to receiving the final locked dataset.
Study Included	MCLA-128-CL01
Population Included	All patients that received at least one dose of zenocutuzumab in study MCLA-128-CL01.
Endpoint	Occurrence of a treatment related adverse event of grade 3 or higher (TRAE grade \ge3)
Occurrence of an adverse event irrespective of causality of grade 3 or higher (Any AE grade \ge3)
Occurrence of any treatment related adverse event (TRAE any grade)
Occurrence of an infusion related reaction after the first dose (IRR first dose)
Occurrence of diarrhea (Diarrhea)
Occurrence of clinically significant left ventricular ejection fraction reduction (LVEF reduction)
Time after first dose to first occurrence of an adverse event irrespective of causality of grade 3 or higher (time to Any AE grade \ge3)
Time after the first dose to first occurrence of a dose interruption (time to dose interruption)

No. of Patients (total, and with individual PK)	330, all with individual PK.
Population Characteristics (Table 57)	General	Age 61 (21 - 88) years.
Weight 67 (38.4 – 125.5)kg
132 (40%) male, 198 (60%) female
238 (72%) caucasian, 67 (20%) Asian, 12 (3.6%) not reported, 7 (2.1%) other, 6 (1.8%) black or African American.
Organ impairment	ALB: median 39.0 (range 20.0-49.0) g/L
AST: 27.0 (11.0-206) IU/L
ALP: 108 (35.0-1062) IU/L
ALT: 19.0 (4.00-226) IU/L
BILI: 8.55 (2.22-30.8) \muM
CREAT: 67.2 (31.8-153) \mumol/L
Pediatrics (if any)	Not included
Geriatrics (if any)	Not included
Dose(s) Included	Part 1: 40 to 900 mg Q3W
L1881: Part 2: 750 mg Q3W, 400 mg Q1W (800 mg loading dose) and 750 mg Q2W
Exposure Metrics Explored (range)	Cavg,ss: median 74.1 (range 1.05- 174) µg/mL.
Cmax after first dose: median 223 (range 13.1 - 456) µg/mL.
Cmax at steady state: median 243 (range 13.1 – 554 ) µg/mL.
Covariates Evaluated	Age, sex, baseline bodyweight, race, ECOG status, liver enzyme category, tumour tpe, sum of target lesion diameters, presence of visceral lesions, number of organs involved, number of previous lines of therapy, dosing regimen and study part and dosing regimen.
Final Model Parameters	Summary	Acceptability [FDA's comments]
Model Structure	
For TRAE grade ≥3, Any AE grade ≥3, TRAE any grade, IRR first dose, Diarrhea and LVEF reduction a logistic regression model was applied:
\logit(P) = \log_e\left(\frac{P}{1-P}\right) \approx \text{intercept} + \text{slope} \cdot \text{exposure}
where P is the predicted probability of response, intercept is the estimated probability of response when the exposure metric is zero, and the slope is the estimated slope of the linear relationship with exposure.
For time to Any AE grade ≥3 and time to dose interruption a Kaplan-Meier evaluation was performed.
Acceptable
Model Parameter Estimates	Table 61	Acceptable
Model Evaluation	
For TRAE grade ≥3, TRAE any grade, Diarrhea and LVEF reduction no significant relationship with exposure was identified.
Probability for Any AE grade ≥3 and IRR after the first dose were found to decrease with increasing exposure.
Acceptable
Covariates and Clinical Relevance	TRAE grade ≥3, TRAE any grade, Diarrhea and LVEF reduction: no significant relationship with exposure, therefore multivariate analysis including covariates could not be performed.
Any AE grade ≥3: decreasing probability with increasing Cavg,ss. In addition, significant effects of number of organs involved, number of previous lines of therapy and Asian patients, where patients with 2-3 therapies and non-Asian patients had a higher probability for this event.
IRR first dose: decreasing probability with increasing Cmax1st.
Acceptable
Simulation for Specific Population	Not applicable	Acceptable
L1882: Visualization of E-R relationships	Figure 20, Figure 21 and Figure 22.	Acceptable
Overall Clinical Relevance for ER	
No relationship between exposure and TRAE grade \ge 3, TRAE any grade, Diarrhea and LVEF reduction was identified.
A negative relationship between Any AE grade \ge 3 and exposure may possibly be because of early dropout of patients before reaching their potential C_{\text{avg,ss}}.
For infusion-related reactions (IRRs) after the first dose, the planned analysis was considered not to be informative, because the occurrence of this event triggered the interruption of the infusion, thus leading to a lower C_{\text{max,1st}} on average by design.
Acceptable

L1896: ## 19.4.2.7. Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses
L1897: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1898: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1899: The benefit-risk profile of the proposed dosage of zenocutuzumab 750 mg IV Q2W is supported by the available data.
L1900: Positive trend for E-R relationship between exposure (C_{avg,ss}) and response (overall response rate &amp; duration of response) was identified based on 129 patients from study MCLA-128-CL01 at one dose level at 750 mg. Interpretation should be cautious due to potential confounding issue and data limitation.
L1901: No positive trend of exposure (C_{avg,ss} and C_{max,ss}) for TRAE grade\ge3, TRAE any grade, diarrhea and LVEF reduction was identified based on 330 patients from study MCLA-128-CL01.

L1918: Figure 2 displays four dose-response curves showing the inhibition of heterodimer formation by various antibodies. The Y-axis represents Relative Light Units (RLU) and the X-axis represents Antibody concentration (nM) on a logarithmic scale (0.001 to 100 nM). Anti-TT (gray circles) is used as a negative control, showing minimal RLU across all concentrations.
The four panels show:
EGFR/HER2 heterodimer formation: MCLA-128 (blue circles) shows strong inhibition, with RLU dropping significantly below the control curve starting around 0.1 nM. Other controls (anti-HER2 parental, anti-HER3 parental, trastuzumab analog, pertuzumab analog, AMG888 analog, Perjeta) show minimal inhibition.
HER2/HER3 heterodimer formation: MCLA-128 (blue circles) shows strong inhibition, with RLU dropping significantly below the control curve starting around 0.1 nM. Other controls show minimal inhibition.
HER2/HER4 heterodimer formation: MCLA-128 (blue circles) shows strong inhibition, with RLU dropping significantly below the control curve starting around 0.1 nM. Other controls show minimal inhibition.
EGFR/HER3 heterodimer formation: MCLA-128 (blue circles) shows strong inhibition, with RLU dropping significantly below the control curve starting around 0.1 nM. Other controls show minimal inhibition.
In all four panels, MCLA-128 demonstrates potent and specific inhibition of HRG-induced HER2:HER3 dimerization, while other tested antibodies do not.
L1919: Figure 3 displays the inhibition of PI3K-AKT signaling and cell survival by MCLA-128 in NRG1-fusion-expressing immortalized HBEC cell lines.
A: Inhibition of CD74-NRG1-induced signaling. Western blot analysis of HBEC-CD74-NRG1 cells treated with increasing concentrations of MCLA-128 (0, 0.1, 1, 10, 100, 1000 nM). The blot shows bands for P-HER3, P-EGFR, EGFR, P-HER4, P-AKT, P-P70 T389, P70, P-ERK, P-MEK1/2, MEK1/2, and GAPDH. MCLA-128 treatment leads to a dose-dependent reduction in phosphorylation of HER3, EGFR, HER4, AKT, and P70 T389, while EGFR, P70, ERK, MEK1/2, and GAPDH levels remain relatively unchanged.
B: Inhibition of viability of cells expressing NRG1 fusions. Three panels show Relative viability (%) versus Drug concentration (\muM) for three cell lines: HBEC-DNP53 (top), HBEC-CD74-NRG1 (middle), and HBEC-VAMP2-NRG1 (bottom). In all panels, MCLA-128 (blue circles) shows dose-dependent inhibition of cell viability, comparable to Afatinib (red triangles). Trastuzumab (green squares) and Erlotinib (orange diamonds) show minimal or no inhibition of viability in these NRG1-fusion-expressing cells.
L1920: Figure 4 illustrates the inhibition of PI3K/AKT signaling and cell survival by MCLA-128 in three NRG1-fusion-expressing pancreatic cell models: H6C7-ATP1B1-NRG1, H6C7-SLC3A2-NRG1, and H6C7 (control).
A: Western Blot Analysis of PI3K/AKT Signaling. H6C7 cell lines expressing empty vector (EV), ATP1B1-NRG1, and SLC3A2-NRG1 were treated with increasing concentrations of MCLA-128 (0 to 1000 nM). The blots show phosphorylation status of key signaling molecules: P-HER3 Y1289, P-AKT S473, AKT, P-EGFR Y1068, P-PRAS40 T246, PRAS40, P-ERK, and ERK. In all three cell lines, MCLA-128 treatment significantly reduced the phosphorylation of HER3, AKT, and PRAS40, indicating inhibition of PI3K/AKT signaling.
B: Western Blot Analysis of NRG1-Fusion-Induced Signaling. H6C7-ATP1B1-NRG1 and H6C7-SLC3A2-NRG1 cells were treated with increasing concentrations of MCLA-128 (0 to 1000 nM). The blots show phosphorylation status of EGFR (Y1086, Y1196), HER2, HER3, HER4, STAT3, MEK1/2, ERK, P70, and S6. In both NRG1-fusion-expressing cell lines, MCLA-128 treatment significantly reduced the phosphorylation of EGFR, HER2, HER3, STAT3, MEK1/2, ERK, P70, and S6, indicating inhibition of NRG1-fusion-induced signaling.
C: Inhibition of Viability. The relative cell growth (%) is plotted against the concentration of MCLA-128 (\muM) and Afatinib (a control EGFR/HER2 inhibitor) for three cell lines: H6C7 (top), H6C7-ATP1B1-NRG1 (middle), and H6C7-SLC3A2-NRG1 (bottom). In all three cell lines, MCLA-128 treatment significantly inhibited cell growth in a dose-dependent manner, with EC50 values ranging from 1.2\times 10^{-4} \muM to 2.3\times 10^{-5} \muM. Afatinib also inhibited cell growth, but its potency was generally lower than MCLA-128 in the NRG1-fusion-expressing cells.
L1921: Figure 5 shows two graphs comparing the proliferation index (A.U.) versus IgG concentration (nM) for JIMT-1 and SK-BR-3 breast cancer cell lines stimulated with 12.5 nM HRG. The proliferation index is calculated by dividing the percentage of cells in the G0/G1 and S phase by the percentage of cells in the G2/M phase. The legend indicates three conditions: Neg. Ctrl. (Anti-tetanus toxoid negative control antibody), T*+P* (equimolar combination of trastuzumab and pertuzumab analogs), and MCLA-128. In both cell lines, MCLA-128 shows superior inhibitory activity compared to T*+P* and Neg. Ctrl., especially at low concentrations (10^-4 to 10^-2 nM). For JIMT-1, MCLA-128 reduces proliferation index from approximately 1.3 to 1.05. For SK-BR-3, MCLA-128 reduces proliferation index from approximately 0.8 to 0.25.

L1923: Figure 7 displays four panels (A, B, C, D) illustrating the mechanism of action of MCLA-128 in a HER2-overexpressing tumor xenograft model (JIMT-1).
(A) JIMT-1 xenograft growth curves: Tumor volume (mm3) is plotted against Treatment duration (days). The Vehicle group shows rapid tumor growth, reaching approximately 500 mm3 by day 25. The MCLA-128 group shows significantly inhibited tumor growth, remaining below 200 mm3 throughout the 25-day treatment period. Statistical significance is indicated by asterisks (* p<0.05, *** p<0.005) comparing tumor size between the two groups at various time points.
(B) Phospho-Akt-Ser473 expression: Bar chart showing the Fluorescence Phospho/Akt-Ser473 per mg protein ratio. MCLA-128 treatment significantly reduces Phospho-Akt-Ser473 expression compared to PBS treatment, regardless of the dosing frequency (4 x qwk or 2 x qwk). Statistical significance (** p<0.01) is indicated for both dosing regimens.
(C) HER2/HER3 dimer: Scatter plot showing HER2/HER3 dimer (VeraTag score). MCLA-128 treatment significantly reduces the formation of HER2/HER3 dimers compared to the Vehicle group (p=0.045).
(D) HER3/PI3K complex: Scatter plot showing HER3/PI3K interaction (VeraTag score). MCLA-128 treatment significantly reduces the formation of the HER3/PI3K complex compared to the Vehicle group (p=0.027).
L1924: Figure 8 displays antitumor efficacy data for MCLA-128 in three NRG1-fusion-expressing lung cancer models, comparing treatment with MCLA-128 at doses of 2.5 mg/kg, 8 mg/kg, and 25 mg/kg against a Vehicle control group.
A. ST2891 in Nude mice: Tumor growth curves plotted over 21 days. The Vehicle group shows rapid tumor growth, reaching approximately 1500 \text{mm}^3 by Day 21. MCLA-128 treatment significantly inhibits tumor growth. The 25 mg/kg group shows the greatest inhibition, stabilizing tumor volume around 200 \text{mm}^3. The 8 mg/kg group stabilizes around 300 \text{mm}^3, and the 2.5 mg/kg group stabilizes around 400 \text{mm}^3.
B. ST3204 in Nude mice: Tumor growth curves plotted over 28 days. The Vehicle group shows rapid tumor growth, reaching approximately 800 \text{mm}^3 by Day 28. MCLA-128 treatment significantly inhibits tumor growth. The 25 mg/kg group shows the greatest inhibition, stabilizing tumor volume around 100 \text{mm}^3. The 8 mg/kg group stabilizes around 150 \text{mm}^3, and the 2.5 mg/kg group stabilizes around 200 \text{mm}^3.
C. LUAD-0061AS3 in NOD/SCID mice: Tumor growth curves plotted over 28 days. The Vehicle group shows rapid tumor growth, reaching approximately 1000 \text{mm}^3 by Day 28. MCLA-128 treatment significantly inhibits tumor growth. The 25 mg/kg group shows the greatest inhibition, stabilizing tumor volume around 100 \text{mm}^3. The 8 mg/kg group stabilizes around 200 \text{mm}^3, and the 2.5 mg/kg group stabilizes around 300 \text{mm}^3.
In all three models, MCLA-128 treatment demonstrates dose-dependent antitumor efficacy, with the 25 mg/kg dose showing the most potent inhibition of tumor growth.
